NATIONAL DISABILITY INSURANCE SCHEME ACT 2013 (Cth)
SECTION 73ZN BANNING ORDER-  STATEMENT OF REASONS
This is a statement of reasons for the making of a Banning Order on 20 December 2022 against 
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 
2013 (NDIS Act).
Procedural steps
I refer to my letter dated 19 October 2022 advising of the proposed revocation of the registration of 
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the Provider”) and the relevant facts and matters 
that I have considered in reaching the view the Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is likely 
to contravene the NDIS Act. 
I also gave the Provider the opportunity to make submissions about this due no later than 5.00pm 
(AWST) on 16 November 2022.
I received a request for an extension to that timeframe and granted additional time for submissions 
through to 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).
I acknowledge the submission sent by Mr Tom Monks, Principal Director, Tom & Co Legal Pty Ltd to 
the Commission on 30 November 2022. The Commission has carefully considered the contents of 
the submission to determine whether to revoke the registration of the Provider.
As detailed in a letter of 20 December 2022, I have made a decision to revoke the registration of the 
Provider, commencing from the date of the notice. 
As I have revoked the registration of the Provider, I have made the decision to make a Banning Order 
against the Provider, as provided for by section 73ZN(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.
The relevant facts and information held by the Commission in forming the view to revoke the 
registration and make a Banning Order against the Provider are set out below.
Relevant facts and information
1.
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is a registered NDIS provider, registration 4-4GHG-1073.
2.
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (the Provider) is registered to provide the following services:
Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
Item 20 – Household tasks.
3.
It is alleged that the Provider is providing supports to participants that the provider is not
registered to provide. The Forward Focused Group website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au)
in its ‘Service Offerings’ offered SDA (Specialist Disability Accommodation) at three suburb
locations. The Forward Focused Group Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July
2022 and 28 September 2022 with the SDA service offering.
4.
Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the safety
and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission also holds
information that suggests that the Provider does not support individuals to raise any complaints
they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation from the Provider or the key
personnel.
1406
T73


===== PAGE 237 =====

ATTACHMENT B
NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
Page 5 of 8
5.
This information strongly indicates that the Provider does not have a complaints management
and resolution system in place that ensures that complaints are welcomed, acknowledged,
respected and well-managed.
6.
The Commission holds information that the Provider does not employ workers who hold the
relevant qualifications for their roles. The FFG website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in
its ‘Our Team’ section stated that Ms Sally See was employed by Forward Focused Group as a
Counsellor. Ms See does not hold qualifications with either the Australian Counselling
Association (www.theaca.net.au) or with the Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency
(www.ahpra.gov.au). The Commission does not hold any records of contact information or
worker screening conducted for Ms See.
7.
The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with respect for individual
rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with
applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022, participants including Corina
Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sought to amend or cease service agreements
with the Provider. The written email engagement with the Provider indicated non-compliance
with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and included both
veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,
a.
Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant funds
and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant confirmed, in
writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds from the existing
manager the Founding Executive Director writes “we will be terminating your services with
us effective immediately”.
b.
Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane Clulow
advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the participant
was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.
c.
On 1 July 2022, the Provider responds to an account statement from a supplier stating that,
“we will no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her contract with us.”
d.
Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham ceasing
some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss their concerns. By
email dated 19 June, the Provider through Vida Reid purports to cease support coordination
and terminate the contract for all services with immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing
abusive manner of our staff by yourself and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email
includes a perceived threat that invoices are to be paid immediately or be reported to the
NDIS.
e.
Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, provides that the
support worker has a non-compete and non-disclosure contract which will prevent her for
working privately for the participant. It also states that if the participant had “come to us in
good faith…I would have said okay.”
f.
Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would like all of
your outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to the NDIS”.
8.
The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with integrity, honesty and
transparency.
9.
It is alleged that Forward Focused Group knowingly submitted false claims for payments to the
National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly causing a loss
to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The Commission is aware of
questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.
10. The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on dates
known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider generated
1407
T73

ATTACHMENT B
NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission

invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the necessary 
Plans or Services Agreements in place. 
11. The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
show that the Founding Executive Director is withholding information from other staff of the
Provider on potentially fraudulent invoices. By way of example,
a.
Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from former employee to the Chief Financial Officer raising
questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for participant that
Forward Focused Group had not provided.
12. The Commission holds records of correspondence between the Provider and participants which
reflects a lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-payment or
broken trust as grounds for the Provider to immediately terminate engagements with
participants. By way of example,
a.
Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due to
“serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The Commission holds
a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated 27 March 2022, requesting
that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the Provider cease.
13. The Commission holds information that the Provider has not taken all reasonable steps to
prevent and respond to exploitation of people with disability.
14. The Commission holds information that the Provider is seeking to act as both the service
provider and the authorized person on behalf of a participant to engage with the NDIA in
relation to support provision and funding decisions. By way of example,
a.
The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM Consent
Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward Focused staff member
(contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review of NDIA decisions and manage
complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.
15. The nature of the information held by the Commission strongly suggests to me that the Provider
has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act (as provided for by
section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).
16. The NDIS Act s73J requires providers to comply with registration conditions. It is a condition of
registration that providers comply with the Rules. The National Disability Insurance Scheme
(Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the Practice Standards Rules) section
7(1) requires providers to be registered to provide Specialist Disability Accommodation (SDA).
17. The Provider is clearly advertising the provision of supports that you are not registered to
supply.
18. The allegations and information held by the Commission indicate a significant amount of false
claims against the Scheme, that could have otherwise have been directed to fund essential
supports and services to people with disability.
19. The Provider’s practice of billing multiple NDIS participants for supports and services not
provided compromised the integrity of the Scheme and deprived these NDIS participants of
receiving necessary supports and services that they relied upon and were entitled to receive.
20. The information available to the Commission regarding the way in which the Provider handles
complaints also suggests that the Provider has failed its obligation under the NDIS Practice
Standards to implement a complaints management and resolution system that ensures that
complaints made by all parties are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed. My
view, based on the information available to the Commission, is that Provider does not provide a
supportive environment for any person who makes complaints.

Submissions
21. The Commission received a submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).
22. Your submission notes that a request for further time and further information were not
responded to however, the Commission did respond to this request. This is evidenced by the
Commission’s acceptance of the submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission
date).
23. Your submission responds to a number of facts and information we raised in the notice of
intention to the provider dated 19 October 2022.
24. Your submission confirms that the Provider held relevant information in relation to the issues
identified.
25. Your submission denies all allegations.
26. I have carefully considered the information provided in your submissions alongside previous
information outlined in the letter dated 19 October 2022. I note your submission does not
provide the Commission with additional evidence to support your position.
27. As part of my considerations I am obliged to consider the NDIS Act. Section 3 of the NDIS Act
contains the objects of the NDIS Act, which includes:
a.
Supporting the independence and social and economic participation of people with
disability.
b.
Enabling people with disability to exercise choice and control in the pursuit of their
goals and the panning and delivery of their supports.
c.
Protecting and preventing people with disability from experiencing harm arising
from poor quality or unsafe supports or services provided under the National
Disability Insurance Scheme.
28. In giving effect to the objects of the NDIS Act, including those outlined above, section 3(3)(b)
requires regard to be had to the need to ensure financial sustainability of the National Disability
Insurance Scheme (NDIS). Similarly, as set out in NDIS Act section 4(17) it is the intention of the
Parliament that the Commissioner, amongst others, have regard to the need to ensure financial
sustainability of the NDIS in performing functions and exercising powers under the NDIS Act.
29. As a registered NDIS provider, Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, elected to participate in a
national scheme aimed at assisting those who have permanent and significant disability, and
providing those participants with choice and control over their supports and services, and their
selection of the providers of those supports and services.
30. My view is that the need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be
particularly vulnerable, and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs the Provider’s interest in
continuing to provide supports or services to people with disability.
31. The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and quality
of care expected from NDIS providers when providing supports or services to people with
disability.
32. The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time over which this conduct
took place and continued as well as the instances of similar misconduct against multiple NDIS
participants suggests that Provider’s conduct was systemic in nature.
33. I am also of the view that allowing the Provider to continue providing supports or services to
people with disability would jeopardise public trust in the NDIS.

34. I consider the alleged conduct of the Provider is entirely relevant to my assessment of the
Provider’s suitability to be involved in the provision of supports or services to NDIS participants,
in considering the objects and principles of the NDIS Act.  This is particularly the case as the
parties impacted by the Provider’s actions are NDIS participants.
35. Having weighed all the evidence at hand I reasonably believe that the Provider, has contravened,
is contravening, or is proposing to contravene, the Act.
36. I am therefore satisfied that the power to revoke the registration is available to me and that it is,
in all of the circumstances, a risk responsive and proportionate response. I am therefore also
satisfied that the power to issue a Banning Order is available to me.
37. For the avoidance of doubt, the Banning Order will prevent the Provider from having any
involvement whether directly or indirectly in the delivery, coordination and management of
NDIS-funded supports and services to people with disability.
38. I consider that a timeframe of 10 years is appropriate and will provide a strong level of
protection for people with disability.

Notice of Suspension of your provider registration (4-4GHG-1073) 
under section 73N of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013
with immediate effect.
The National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act) gives me, as the Commissioner’s 
delegate, the power to suspend the registration of a registered NDIS provider for a specified period, 
no longer than thirty (30) days.  
I have made the decision to suspend the registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, registration 
number 4-4GHG-1073, for a period of 30 days, commencing from the date of this notice.
I have suspended your registration as a registered NDIS provider because I reasonably believe that 
you have contravened sections 73V (the NDIS Code of Conduct), 73B (Requirement to be a 
registered NDIS provider) and 73J (compliance with conditions of your registration) of the NDIS Act, 
as provided for by section 73N(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.
The suspension means your registration ceases to have effect until 19 November 2022. The reasons 
for my decision to suspend your registration are attached to this notice (Annexure A).
NDIS Provider Register
Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register 
which must include certain information for each person who is a registered NDIS provider.  This 
includes if the registration of a person is suspended, information about the suspension. As I have 
decided to suspend your registration with immediate effect, this information will be included on the 
NDIS Provider Register, and published online.
Review under section 100 of the NDIS Act
You may request a review of my decision to suspend your registration within 3 months from the 
date that the suspension takes effect.
Any application you may make for an internal review would be dealt with by someone other than 
me, the original decision-maker.

the NDIS Act.
Non-compliance with the suspension
Please note that engaging in conduct that breaches the suspension of registration may be a 
contravention of section 73B of the NDIS Act, insofar as the NDIS rules may require that specified 
classes of supports provided under a participant’s plans be provided only by NDIS providers who are 
registered to provide those classes of supports. A contravention of section 73B attract a civil penalty 
up to $55,500 for individuals.

===== PAGE 249 =====

Annexure A
NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
3
Reasons for the decision to suspend your registration
Legislative provisions
Section 73J of the NDIS Act provides that registered NDIS providers must comply with conditions of 
registration. The conditions they are subject to include those provided at section 73F of the NDIS 
Act. 
Section 73F of the NDIS Act states that the registration of a person as a registered NDIS provider is 
subject to a number of conditions. These conditions include:
•
Section 73F(2)(b) - a condition that the person comply with all applicable requirements of 
the NDIS Code of Conduct;
•
Section 73F(2)(c) - a condition that the person comply with all applicable standards and 
other requirements of the NDIS Practice Standards;
Section 73T(1) of the NDIS Act states that the National Disability Insurance Scheme rules may make 
provision for or in relation to standards concerning the quality of supports or services to be provided 
by registered NDIS providers. 
NDIS Code of Conduct
The NDIS Code of Conduct is set out in section 6 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of 
Conduct) Rules 2018 (Code of Conduct Rules). Relevantly, Code-covered persons must, when 
providing supports or services to people with disability:
•
act with respect for individual rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and
decision-making in accordance with applicable laws and conventions (section 6(a));
•
act with integrity, honesty and transparency (section 6(d));
•
take all reasonable steps to prevent and respond to all forms of violence against, and
exploitation, neglect and abuse of, people with disability (section 6(f)).
Practice Standards
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is registered to provide the following classes of supports under the 
National Disability Insurance Scheme (Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the 
Practice Standards Rules):
•
Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements
•
Item 20 – Household tasks.
In order to remain registered for the above classes of supports, Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd must 
comply with the Practice Standards outlined in Schedule 8 of the Practice Standards Rules. 
Relevant facts and circumstances
I have considered the following matters that give rise to the suspension:
1.
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is a registered NDIS provider, registration number 4-4GHG-
1073, trading as ‘Forward Focused’ (ABN 98643878639).
2.
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is registered to provide the following services:
1419
T77


===== PAGE 250 =====

NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
4
•
Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
•
Item 20 – Household tasks.
3.
It is alleged that Forward Focused Group is providing supports to participants that the
provider is not registered to provide. The FFG website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au)
in its ‘Service Offerings’ offers SDA (Specialist Disability Accommodation) at three suburb
locations. The Forward Focus Group Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July
2022 and 28 September 2022 with the SDA service offering.
4.
Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the
safety and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission also
holds information that suggests that Forward Focused Group does not support individuals to
raise any complaints they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation
from Forward Focused Group or its directors and key personnel.
5.
This information strongly indicates that Forward Focused Group does not have a complaints
management and resolution system in place that ensures that complaints are welcomed,
acknowledged, respected and well-managed.
6.
The Commission holds information that Forward Focused Group does not employ workers
who hold the relevant qualifications for their roles. The website
(www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in its ‘Our Team’ section states that Ms Sally See was
employed by Forward Focused Group as a Counsellor. Ms See does not hold qualifications
with either the Australian Counselling Association (www.theaca.net.au) or with the
Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency (www.ahpra.gov.au). The Commission
could not locate any records of contact information or worker screening conducted for Ms
See.
7.
The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with respect for individual
rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with
applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022, participants including Corina
Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sort to amend or cease service agreements
with the Provider. The written email engagement with the Provider indicated non-
compliance with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and
included both veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,
a.
Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant
funds and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant
confirmed, in writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds
from the existing manager the Founding Executive Director writes “we will be
terminating your services with us effective immediately”.
b.
Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane
Clulow advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the
participant was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.
c.
On 1 July 2022, the Provider responds to an account statement from a supplier
stating that, ““we will no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her
contract with us.”
d.
Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham
ceasing some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss
their concerns. By email dated 19 June, the Provider through Vida Reid purports to
cease support coordination and terminate the contract for all services with
immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff by yourself

and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email includes a perceived threat that

8.
The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with integrity, honesty and
transparency.
It is alleged that Forward Focused Group are knowingly submitting false claims for payments
to the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly
causing a loss to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The
Commission is aware of questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.
The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on
dates known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider
generated invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the
necessary Plans or Services Agreements in place.
The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
show that the Founding Executive Director is withholding information from other staff of the
Provider on potentially fraudulent invoices. By way of example,
a.
Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from a former employee to the Chief Financial
Officer raising questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for
participant that Forward Focused Group had not provided.
The Commission holds records of correspondence between the Provider and participants 
which reflects a lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-
payment or broken trust as grounds for the Provider to immediately terminate engagements 
with participants. By way of example,
a.
Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due
to “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The
Commission holds a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated
27 March 2022, requesting that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the
Provider cease.
9.
The Commission holds information that the Provider has not taken all reasonable steps to
prevent and respond to exploitation of people with disability.
The Commission holds information that the Provider is seeking to act as both the service
provider and the authorized person on behalf of a participant to engage with the NDIA in
relation to support provision and funding decisions. By way of example,
a.
The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM
Consent Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward
1421
T77


===== PAGE 252 =====

NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
6
Focused staff member (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review 
of NDIA decisions and manage complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.
Concerns about contraventions of the NDIS Act
1.
The nature of the information held by the Commission strongly suggests to me that the
Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act (as
provided for by section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).
2.
The NDIS Act 73J requires provides to comply with registration conditions. It is a condition of
registration that providers comply with the Rules. The National Disability Insurance Scheme
(Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the Practice Standards Rules)
section 7(1) requires providers to be registered to provide Specialist Disability
Accommodation (SDA). The provider is clearly advertising the provision of supports that you
are not registered to supply.
3.
The allegations and information held by the Commission indicate a significant amount of
false claims against the Scheme, that could have otherwise have been directed to fund
essential supports and services to people with disability.
4.
The Provider’s practice of billing multiple NDIS participants for supports and services not
provided compromised the integrity of the Scheme and deprived these NDIS participants of
receiving necessary supports and services that they relied upon and were entitled to receive.
5.
The information available to the Commission regarding the way in which the Provider
handles complaints also suggests that the Provider has failed its obligation under the NDIS
Practice Standards to implement a complaints management and resolution system that
ensures that complaints made by all parties are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and
well-managed. My preliminary view, based on the information available to the Commission,
is that Provider does not provide a supportive environment for any person who makes
complaints.
6.
The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and
quality of care expected from NDIS providers when providing supports or services to people
with disability.
7.
The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time over which this
alleged conduct took place and continued as well as the instances of similar misconduct
against multiple NDIS participants suggests that Provider’s conduct was systemic in nature.
8.
My preliminary view is that the need to protect the interests of people with disability, who
may be particularly vulnerable, and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs the Provider’s
interest in remaining registered in these circumstances. I am also of the view that allowing
the Provider to remain registered in the circumstances would jeopardise public trust in the
Scheme.
9.
Having considered the functions of the Commissioner, the objects of the NDIS Act, and the
need to consider the financial sustainability of the NDIS in exercising the Commissioner’s
powers under the NDIS Act, I consider that the registration of the Provider should be
suspended under section 73N(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.
1422
T77


===== PAGE 253 =====

NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
7
In deciding whether to suspend Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd registration, I have had regard to the 
following matters as required by section 73N(4) of the NDIS Act:
a)
With regards to the nature, significance and persistence of any contravention, or proposed
contravention, of the NDIS Act (section 73N(4)(a))
•
The conduct of Forward Focused Group towards NDIS participants is very serious and
warrants action to ensure Forward Focused Group cannot continue to operate as a
registered NDIS provider.
•
The evidence available to the Commission strongly suggests that the provider is
committing fraud by charging participants for services not provided, or excessive
amounts. Based on information available to the Commission, it is highly likely that both
Directors of Forward Focused Group (Ms Vida Reid and Mr Simon Reid) and the Chief
Financial Officer (Mr Rejaul Alam) have committed the alleged fraudulent activities.
•
Information held by the Commission also indicates that Forward Focused is providing
supports and services that they are not registered to provide.
•
The information provided by complainants also strongly indicate a pattern of bullying,
harassment and intimidating behaviour displayed by the Founding Executive Director Ms
Reid towards NDIS participants.
b)
With regards to the action that can be taken to address any contravention, or proposed
contravention (section 73N(4)(b)):
•
Given the nature of the conduct described above and noting that it appears to be
intentional and recurrent, the Commission considers it unlikely that the key personnel of
Forward Focused Group will fully implement any corrective action prescribed by the
Commission to address the provider’s contraventions.
c)
With regards to the extent (if any) to which the person is conducting its affairs as a registered
NDIS provider in a way that may cause harm to, or jeopardise, public trust in the NDIS (section
73N(4)(c)):
•
Based on the seriousness of Forward Focused and Vida Reid’s conduct and the potential
impacts on NDIS participants, the Commission considers that there remains a significant
risk to participants if Forward Focused Group was to continue providing supports to
participants.
d)
With regards to the health, safety or wellbeing of people with disability receiving supports or
services from the person (section 73N(4)(d)):
•
Forward Focused breaches of the NDIS Code of Conduct are particularly concerning. For
the reasons detailed above, the Commission remains of the view there is an ongoing need
to protect the interests, safety and wellbeing of vulnerable persons receiving disability
supports and services.
e)
There are no other circumstances considered relevant at this time (section 73N(4)(e)).
1423
T77


===== PAGE 254 =====

NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
8
Having regard to all of the matters set out above, I am satisfied that Forward Focus Group Pty Ltd 
has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene, the NDIS Act (section 73N(1)(a)). 
Taking into account the totality of the circumstances and the regulatory functions of the 
Commissioner I have determined that it is reasonable and proportionate to suspend the registration 
for a period of thirty (30) days under section 73N(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.

Opportunity to make submissions about a proposed revocation of registration under section 
73P(4) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth)
I am a delegate of the Commissioner for the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission 
(the Commissioner).
Proposed revocation of registration and banning order
Section 73P of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth) (NDIS Act) gives me, as the 
Commissioner’s delegate, the power to revoke the registration of a person as a registered NDIS 
provider on the grounds listed in section 73P(1) of the NDIS Act. Relevantly, in relation to this 
matter, I can revoke the registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the 
Provider”) if I am satisfied that it has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the 
NDIS Act (as provided for by section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).
A banning order, which can prohibit or restrict specified activities by an NDIS provider, if the 
Commissioner has revoked the registration of the person as a registered NDIS provider (as provided 
for by section 73ZN(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.
Opportunity to make submissions
Before deciding to revoke the registration of a registered NDIS provider, the Commissioner (or a 
delegate of the Commissioner) must give the person an opportunity to make submissions on the 
matter, in accordance with section 73P(4) of the NDIS Act. A separate submission process in relation 
to any proposed banning order made under section 73ZN(1)(a) is not required as set out in section 
73ZN(8)(b) of the NDIS Act.
The purpose of this letter is to inform the Provider of relevant facts and the matters that I have 
considered in reaching the view the Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to 
contravene the NDIS Act and to give it the opportunity to make a submission about this before I 
make a final decision.

NDIS Provider Register
Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register 
which must include certain information for each person who is a registered NDIS provider. Relevant 
to this matter, section 73ZS(5)(e) permits the NDIS Provider Register to include information about a 
former NDIS provider, including information about the revocation of a person’s registration. 
Should I proceed to determine to revoke the Provider’s registration and make a banning order, 
information about the revocation and banning order will be included on the NDIS Provider Register, 
available on the Commission’s website.
Making a submission
I have not made a final decision in respect of this matter and I will not until the Provider has had an 
opportunity to make submissions in writing to me. This gives the Provider the opportunity to tell me 
anything it believes may be appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this 
matter or to comment on the seriousness of it.
Section 73P(4)(b) of the NDIS Act provides that the Provider may make submissions within 28 days 
after receiving this notice. If no submissions are made within that time period, any revocation may 
take effect as early as 7 days after the end of the 28 day-period. The Provider will be notified in 
writing of any decision made in relation to the proposed revocation.
Any submissions the Provider may wish to make must be received by the Commission within 28 days 
of its receipt of this notice unless it seeks, and is granted, an extension.
